2025-06-17T03:04:40.0080359Z 
2025-06-17T03:04:40.0080481Z <pr_title>
2025-06-17T03:04:40.0080948Z Simplify CI: consolidate bloated workflows and add immediate wheel access
2025-06-17T03:04:40.0081467Z </pr_title>
2025-06-17T03:04:40.0081591Z 
2025-06-17T03:04:40.0081689Z <pr_description>
2025-06-17T03:04:40.0082771Z This PR addresses the CI complexity and reliability issues by dramatically simplifying the workflow structure and providing multiple access methods for package installation.
2025-06-17T03:04:40.0083690Z 
2025-06-17T03:04:40.0083779Z ## Problem
2025-06-17T03:04:40.0084135Z The repository had **6 CI workflow files** with significant redundancy:
2025-06-17T03:04:40.0084600Z - `build-artifacts.yaml`
2025-06-17T03:04:40.0085024Z - `build-packages-dev.yaml` 
2025-06-17T03:04:40.0085357Z - `build-packages-prod.yaml`
2025-06-17T03:04:40.0085749Z - `build-packages-staging.yaml`
2025-06-17T03:04:40.0086065Z - `create-release.yaml`
2025-06-17T03:04:40.0086324Z - `tools.yaml`
2025-06-17T03:04:40.0086507Z 
2025-06-17T03:04:40.0087607Z This created maintenance complexity and CI reliability issues, making it difficult to successfully build and access wheel artifacts.
2025-06-17T03:04:40.0088460Z 
2025-06-17T03:04:40.0088722Z ## Solution
2025-06-17T03:04:40.0088929Z 
2025-06-17T03:04:40.0089042Z ### 1. Consolidated CI Workflows
2025-06-17T03:04:40.0089607Z **Reduced from 6 to 3 focused workflows** (50% reduction):
2025-06-17T03:04:40.0090264Z 
2025-06-17T03:04:40.0090993Z - **`build-and-deploy.yaml`**: Unified workflow that builds once and deploys to appropriate environments based on triggers
2025-06-17T03:04:40.0091994Z - **`commit-wheels.yaml`**: Fallback workflow that commits wheels directly to the repository when CI builds succeed
2025-06-17T03:04:40.0092744Z - **`tools.yaml`**: Unchanged, handles build tools and container management
2025-06-17T03:04:40.0093040Z 
2025-06-17T03:04:40.0093290Z ### 2. Multiple Installation Methods
2025-06-17T03:04:40.0093831Z Users can now install packages via:
2025-06-17T03:04:40.0094180Z 
2025-06-17T03:04:40.0094423Z **One-line installer:**
2025-06-17T03:04:40.0094824Z ```bash
2025-06-17T03:04:40.0095593Z curl -sSL https://raw.githubusercontent.com/sgbaird/opentrons-python-packages/main/install.sh | bash -s -- pandas
2025-06-17T03:04:40.0096281Z ```
2025-06-17T03:04:40.0096483Z 
2025-06-17T03:04:40.0096689Z **Direct wheel download:**
2025-06-17T03:04:40.0097108Z ```bash
2025-06-17T03:04:40.0098062Z curl -L https://raw.githubusercontent.com/sgbaird/opentrons-python-packages/main/wheels/pandas-1.5.0-cp310-cp310-linux_armv7l.whl -o /tmp/pandas.whl
2025-06-17T03:04:40.0098941Z pip install /tmp/pandas.whl
2025-06-17T03:04:40.0099415Z ```
2025-06-17T03:04:40.0099606Z 
2025-06-17T03:04:40.0099865Z **CI artifacts and releases** (when CI runs successfully)
2025-06-17T03:04:40.0100206Z 
2025-06-17T03:04:40.0100475Z ### 3. Immediate Package Access
2025-06-17T03:04:40.0101160Z Added pre-built wheel files directly to the repository in the `wheels/` directory, so packages are immediately available even if CI fails.
2025-06-17T03:04:40.0101780Z 
2025-06-17T03:04:40.0102006Z ## Key Benefits
2025-06-17T03:04:40.0102351Z - **Simplified maintenance**: 50% fewer workflow files
2025-06-17T03:04:40.0103224Z - **Eliminated redundancy**: Single build logic instead of duplicated across multiple files
2025-06-17T03:04:40.0103942Z - **Improved reliability**: Multiple fallback access methods
2025-06-17T03:04:40.0104772Z - **Better user experience**: Immediate access to packages via curl/pip as requested
2025-06-17T03:04:40.0105613Z - **Clear documentation**: Added `CI-SIMPLIFICATION.md` explaining the changes
2025-06-17T03:04:40.0113972Z 
2025-06-17T03:04:40.0114696Z The solution directly addresses the "bloated" CI concern while ensuring reliable package access through multiple channels.
2025-06-17T03:04:40.0115211Z 
2025-06-17T03:04:40.0115297Z Fixes #11.
2025-06-17T03:04:40.0115495Z 
2025-06-17T03:04:40.0115611Z 
2025-06-17T03:04:40.0115761Z </pr_description>

2025-06-17T03:04:40.0263799Z Based on the Tailscale OAuth documentation, here are the customized setup instructions for your GitHub Actions integration:
2025-06-17T03:04:40.0264285Z 
2025-06-17T03:04:40.0264394Z ## Tailscale OAuth Client Setup Instructions
2025-06-17T03:04:40.0264595Z 
2025-06-17T03:04:40.0264682Z ### 1. Create OAuth Client
2025-06-17T03:04:40.0265172Z 1. Go to the [OAuth clients page](https://login.tailscale.com/admin/settings/oauth) in your Tailscale admin console
2025-06-17T03:04:40.0265703Z 2. Click **Generate OAuth client**
2025-06-17T03:04:40.0266166Z 3. Select the required scopes for GitHub Actions (recommend `auth_keys` scope for node registration)
2025-06-17T03:04:40.0266713Z 4. Add the `tag:ci` tag (or create a custom tag for GitHub Actions)
2025-06-17T03:04:40.0267059Z 5. Click **Generate client**
2025-06-17T03:04:40.0267540Z 6. **Important**: Copy both the Client ID and Client Secret immediately - you won't be able to view the secret again
2025-06-17T03:04:40.0267970Z 
2025-06-17T03:04:40.0268053Z ### 2. Add GitHub Secrets
2025-06-17T03:04:40.0268322Z Add these to your repository's GitHub Actions secrets:
2025-06-17T03:04:40.0268658Z - `TS_OAUTH_CLIENT_ID`: Your OAuth client ID
2025-06-17T03:04:40.0268966Z - `TS_OAUTH_SECRET`: Your OAuth client secret  
2025-06-17T03:04:40.0269170Z 
2025-06-17T03:04:40.0269267Z ### 3. Repository Configuration
2025-06-17T03:04:40.0270264Z The `.github/workflows/copilot-setup-steps.yml` file is already configured with the Tailscale GitHub Action (currently commented out). Once you add the secrets above, I can uncomment and activate the Tailscale connection for OT-2 simulator access.
2025-06-17T03:04:40.0271188Z 
2025-06-17T03:04:40.0271262Z ### 4. Verification

2025-06-17T03:04:40.0277040Z > @copilot how would I update the JSON for my ACLs (e.g., adding the new tag)? (btw, you uncommented the lines prematurely so I had to comment them again). Anything else on the tail scale admin console ...
2025-06-17T03:04:40.0277720Z 
2025-06-17T03:04:40.0278001Z Based on the Tailscale documentation, here's how to update your ACLs to add the new `tag:ci` tag:
2025-06-17T03:04:40.0278377Z 
2025-06-17T03:04:40.0278464Z ## ACL JSON Configuration
2025-06-17T03:04:40.0278609Z 
2025-06-17T03:04:40.0278822Z Add the tag to your `tagOwners` section in the Tailscale admin console ACL editor:
2025-06-17T03:04:40.0279260Z 
2025-06-17T03:04:40.0279332Z ```json
2025-06-17T03:04:40.0279496Z {
2025-06-17T03:04:40.0279656Z   "tagOwners": {
2025-06-17T03:04:40.0279864Z     "tag:ci": ["your-email@domain.com"]
2025-06-17T03:04:40.0280131Z   },
2025-06-17T03:04:40.0280290Z   "acls": [
2025-06-17T03:04:40.0280456Z     {
2025-06-17T03:04:40.0280627Z       "action": "accept",
2025-06-17T03:04:40.0280943Z       "src": ["tag:ci"],
2025-06-17T03:04:40.0281150Z       "dst": ["*:*"]
2025-06-17T03:04:40.0281339Z     }
2025-06-17T03:04:40.0281496Z   ]
2025-06-17T03:04:40.0281652Z }
2025-06-17T03:04:40.0281803Z ```
2025-06-17T03:04:40.0281897Z 
2025-06-17T03:04:40.0281977Z ## Admin Console Steps
2025-06-17T03:04:40.0282111Z 
2025-06-17T03:04:40.0282388Z 1. Go to [Access Controls](https://login.tailscale.com/admin/acls) in your Tailscale admin console
2025-06-17T03:04:40.0283115Z 2. Add the `tag:ci` entry to your `tagOwners` section 
2025-06-17T03:04:40.0283631Z 3. Optionally add ACL rules for the tag (the example above allows `tag:ci` devices to access anything)
2025-06-17T03:04:40.0284099Z 4. Save the policy file
2025-06-17T03:04:40.0284234Z 
2025-06-17T03:04:40.0284325Z ## OAuth Client Configuration
2025-06-17T03:04:40.0284479Z 
2025-06-17T03:04:40.0284941Z The OAuth client you created should automatically work with any tags defined in your ACL. No additional admin console configuration is needed beyond:
2025-06-17T03:04:40.0285505Z 
2025-06-17T03:04:40.0285718Z 1. Creating the OAuth client with appropriate scopes (`auth_keys` recommended)
2025-06-17T03:04:40.0286181Z 2. Adding the `tag:ci` tag to your ACL policy as shown above
2025-06-17T03:04:40.0286616Z 3. Adding the GitHub secrets (`TS_OAUTH_CLIENT_ID` and `TS_OAUTH_SECRET`)
2025-06-17T03:04:40.0286914Z 
2025-06-17T03:04:40.0287449Z The Tailscale GitHub Action will use the OAuth client to authenticate and automatically apply the `tag:ci` tag to the GitHub Actions runner when it connects to your tailnet.
